This is indeed a new business model - the model we are proposing, which already has the first results. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u262	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the impression that, in discussing this point, we forget the merit of this law, namely simplification for the beneficiaries. The Act first refers to changes that improve the safety of applicants, improve legal certainty in contact with intermediate bodies or management bodies. [[Voice from the room: No, it isn't.]] Believe me, I have 17 years of experience in this field, so I'm not speaking politically, I'm just talking about what I think. The Act in contacts with the local government introduces a voivodship with one more power, which is at the moment the task of the Minister responsible for regional development. Namely, he says that the minister of regional development, by making the so-called. the institution's designation, he can eat the voivodship. So here we are not dealing with the taking away of any competence of any local government of the voivodship. If we are talking about the role of the voivode, there are two aspects to this law. Firstly, we are reducing the voivodship's competence by saying that instead of being a member of the monitoring committee, he is an observer. Today, in 13 operational programmes, the voivode is a member of the monitoring committee. After the amendment of the bill, he will only be able to be an observer. The third element in which the voivode appears is the opportunity to participate, at his request, in the work of the evaluation committee as an observer. Let me remind you that being an observer in the project evaluation committee is not the power that emerged today. It is part of the programs that have been implemented. I can say that in the programme that I supervised in previous years, every member of the monitoring committee not only representing institutions, but also one that is, for example. the representative of the trade union, the representative of the employers' organisation, the representative of the NGO on the monitoring committee, may be involved in its work. All decisions that are made in the regional programme are decisions of the managing authority, i.e. the management board of the voivodship. So I really think it's a charge that doesn't have any coverage in reality. If we are talking about making certain procedures obsolete, I would point out that a number of these changes are optional. They say that the institution that organizes the competition, looking at the merit point of view, i.e. whether the competition enjoys the interest of the applicants or not, may optionally introduce certain procedures. Very often the possibility of improving the proposal or cooperation with the beneficiary is also an facilitation for the regional programme in the field of innovation and research. We had very often competitions, where there were about 40% potential applicants in relation to the entire allocation. The Act does not introduce an obligation for each competition, but gives the managing authority the possibility where it considers it appropriate, where it considers it justified, to take advantage of certain possibilities which it gives, for example. to improve, to introduce certain changes in cooperation with the beneficiary or to complement obvious errors. We have dealt with such situations in the past, that when a comma was missing, a dot was missing, the application was rejected, the beneficiary could not obtain funding. Very often in the Seym we heard the question of why applications are rejected when they are signed not in the colour of the pen requested by the institution. Nor is it that the law is aimed at local governments or institutions at regional level. I can also say, on the basis of my experience and contact with national institutions, that they often make the same comments as regional institutions. It is simply re-bureaucratisation that we must all go through, all institutions, both at national and regional level. One last thing, issues relating to progress in implementation. Indeed, unlike the previous financial perspectives, regional programmes are less advanced than central programmes. That hasn't happened yet. A number of programmes certified less than 1% appropriations for the entire financial perspective, with the aim of so-called: n+3 at more than 6% This year. In conclusion, of course, the law introduces a number of changes that will require effort from all institutions that operate in the system and at national level and at regional level. In my deep opinion, it is a law which respects the competence of the various actors involved, especially the local government and the representative of the government in the field of voivodeship, and contributes significantly to improving the certainty of the beneficiary in relations with the managing authority. At the moment, he has to refer very often to a number of different documents that are required of him, he has to familiarize himself with various documents. For him, the basis should be the competitive documentation which is submitted by the institution and the contract in which all rights and obligations should be described. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u373	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have been authorized by the Minister of Energy to answer your question to the Minister of Energy. The national electricity system is a safe system, first of all. The level of capacity reserve available to the transmission system operator, i.e. the Polish Energy Networks, exceeds the required threshold 18% the national peak power demand and the procedures adopted in the event of a safety risk of its operation ensure safe supply of electricity to final customers. The work of the national energy system shall be managed properly and with due diligence. Therefore, the temporary reduction in the generation capacity available to the operator in the second week of August 2015 should be considered as an exceptional situation. At the same time, it can be noted that the need for restrictions on electricity consumption is due to the occurrence of similar unpredictable events, because last year it was indeed such a accumulation of events that combined with the heat that were, and the shortage of water to cool some power blocks led to the need to reduce supply, not stop, but reduce energy supply to some consumers. The Ministry of Energy together with the energy groups, with the operator, takes the necessary steps to be prepared for the worst-case scenarios. However, this does not mean that these adverse developments, as in August 2015, will occur. There is a very low probability of such a scenario. However, in the last year, Polish Energy Networks have taken steps by the operator which, in fact, secure us and this year, and in the following years. First, the cold intervention service was purchased for the period 2016 and 2017 with an option to extend until 2019. The total capacity of production units contracted under this service is 830 MW. Secondly, in December 2015, a new power connection was launched, so it came out, this investment was completed at the end of 2015 and put into service. It is an energy bridge between Poland and Lithuania, LitPol Link. This is a 500 MW connection. Thirdly, at the Polish-German border in June 2016, the operation of phase shifters was started at the southern link between the Polish and German systems, between the station Mikulów in Poland and Hagenwerder in Germany. At the same time, the northern passage, notabene at the request of the German party, was temporarily blocked. These are technical problems from the German side, but in fact, in general it helps us to maintain our national energy system, because it protects us from unwanted forced circular flows that took place in a large number, now it is limited, from the German side to Poland. Fourthly, power reserves in non-centrally controlled generation units have been activated. This service is intended to ensure the minimum, necessary for the work of the national energy system, the amount of installed active power. This leads to a reserve of 300 MW. In addition, the DSR service was launched by PSE, which is a reduction in customer power demand. This may lead to the necessary savings. That's about 300 MW, too. The possibility of energy imports has also been increased if necessary by an operator, which may also do so in emergency cases. All of this together allows the operator to balance the domestic energy demand with generation opportunities, with production. Of course, in every case this necessary excess power is preserved. It can be added that, recently, on 28 June, now, this year the maximum demand of the national energy system in the summit was almost 23 000. MW, 22 875 MW. We handled it pretty well. It can be assumed that these problems that occurred in 2015, now, in 2017, do not threaten us. The operator has a monthly forecast that he's preparing. [[♪ The bell ♪]] We're analyzing her. This forecast for July looks very good. The reserve is preserved. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u381	Mr. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, Thank you very much for this question and I am very pleased to be able to answer it. The first point is what the antiquity protection system will look like now and what the amendment has been used for. Maybe I'll say a few introductory sentences before we get to the National Monument Protection Fund. First of all, the National Monument Protection Fund is an idea that has been discussed in conservators, art historians since the 1970s. We also have further resolutions of the Council for the Protection of Monuments and in all subsequent resolutions of the Council for the Protection of Monuments for long, long years called for the creation of this National Fund for the Protection of Monuments as a non-budget source of financing of monuments. The last meeting of the Council for the Protection of Monuments took place about 3 weeks ago and a resolution was adopted in full support of the idea of creating the National Fund for the Protection of Monuments. The NFOZ will be a state-owned special-purpose fund within the meaning of the Law of 27 August 2009 on public finances, the responsibility of which will be the Minister with responsibility for Culture and the Protection of National Heritage. The NSA will also have a separate bank account and will not have legal personality. The revenue of the NFOZ will be the proceeds from administrative fines and the references to the crimes of destruction or damage to the monument. What are we gonna... Why do we need this NFOZ? As you know, the general conservator of monuments has at his disposal over PLN 100 million for the entire 37 million country. The funds for the protection of Krakow's monuments amount to about 30 million, only for one city, I have 100 million for the whole of Poland. We need money very much, especially in those cases where, as a result of the action of third forces, i.e. simply forces of nature, such as flooding, flooding, storms, monuments are destroyed and cannot be secured even to the most urgent extent. So, first of all, this National Monument Protection Fund will be dedicated to helping monuments that have been destroyed - also as a result of the fire. We have about a dozen fires of historic, registered objects a year. Most of them are wooden objects, although not only. We very much need additional money that could help in this most desirable range. It will also be expenditures to be used for conservation or restoration works on the monument inscribed on the Heritage Treasury List. I remind you that the Heritage Treasure List includes those moving monuments that have the highest rank. As for landmarks, there are monuments of history or e.g. UNESCO, so for these monuments are... They're on the Heritage treasure list. All these objects need additional security that protects, for example. before possible getting wet, before stealing, and this fund will also work to strengthen protection. Thirdly, we have very few replacements across the country. If we have an object that is completely degraded, e.g. it will collapse within a year, and the owner or manager of the facility persistently refuses to follow the recommendations, we will be able to provide assistance from the National Monument Protection Fund to secure this facility. This will consist in some cooperation between the general conservationist of monuments and the provincial conservationists of monuments. Ladies and gentlemen, this action of the public is important because so far the general conservator of monuments could spend money only and exclusively in the competition, and sometimes it happens that the owner or manager does not apply to the competition, even though he has an object of exceptional importance for national heritage. These are our most urgent needs that we will try to meet. I would also like to say that annual revenues to the NFOZ are expected at the level of about PLN 16 million. From the point of view of antiquities protection, every sum is really important and I am very happy that this fund will work. She also asked a question about the entire antiquities protection system. Let me put it this way: as far as the regulation is concerned, the regulation has more than 30 pages. It would have to be pointed out precisely which point of this regulation makes life difficult for conservationists or keepers of monuments. Let me put it this way: of course, we are based on some legal system, it is very important for us, but this problem that we are struggling with when it comes to the protection of monuments in Poland is mainly about the lack of money. We have 76,000. objects entered in the register of monuments, about 800,000. entered in the register of monuments. We have a very low awareness of the protection of national heritage at local government level. This is really important, and with what you said, that in the 1990s it came from state ownership into private hands and we lost a large resource of protected monuments, I completely agree. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u405	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation by Mr Jerzy Materna.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u406	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! The Ministry's plans for the development of inland waterways in Poland for 2016 and 2020 with a view to 2030 are set out, among others. the achievement of an international class of navigability on the Odrzaska Waterway and its inclusion in the European network of waterways. The first step towards this priority is the elimination of bottlenecks, i.e. places that restrict inland navigation. In some cases, narrow throats include infrastructure crossing inland waterways, including bridges whose clearance over the shipping route is too low. In the short term, it is planned to complete the renovation and modernisation work on mucus on the Gliwice Channel, until their efficiency and functionality is restored within the existing class of waterway, and to start the design and modernisation work on the channel stations to obtain class V, if justified, as the future Silesian Channel will run bilaterally to the existing channel. In turn, in the long term, the Gliwice Channel should carry out a gradual reconstruction of the channel stations to Class V, together with the construction of new locks next to the existing ones, if justified. Two projects concerning the Gliwice Canal, implemented by the Regional Water Management Board in Gliwice, have been identified to finance the funding of the Operational Programme. 1. Modernization of Odrzańskie mucus on the Gliwice Channel, on the section on the management board of RZGW Gliwice, adaptation to the third class of water road, stage I, phase II. Project implementation. Phase I was launched in the PAIiIZ in 2007 and 2013 and includes the completion of the modernisation of two mucus on the Gliwice Canal: Dzierżno and Slabęda. The planned date of completion of the project is the fourth quarter of 2018, while the cost is estimated to be over PLN 50 million. 2. Modernization of Odrzańskie mucus on the Gliwice Canal, on the section on the management board of RZGW Gliwice, adaptation to the third class of water road, stage II. As part of the project, two mucus will be upgraded on Gliwice Channel: Nowa Wieś and Sławęcice. The reconstruction and modernisation of buildings and equipment, the creation of a monitoring system and the implementation of water-saving systems were also planned. The planned date of completion of the project is the fourth quarter of 2020, while the estimated cost is over PLN 110 million. The additional scope of work on the Gliwice Canal will be defined by a feasibility study for the modernisation of the Odrzaska Waterway. In order to bridge the Gliwice Canal to restore regular inland navigation, according to preliminary estimates provided by the Regional Water Management Board in Gliwice, approximately PLN 50 million should be spent in the first stage of the work. The financial resources of this amount will enable the removal of demersal deposits classified as dangerous from the most muddy section of Dzierżno near Gliwice and the modernisation of shore reinforcements. Restoring regular inland waterway transport on the Odrzaska Waterway is a priority for the government. It will also increase the volume of cargo transported by inland waterway. According to data from the Polish Energy Group, the construction of new blocks in the Opole power plant will significantly increase the demand for coal from the current 3.5 million tons to 7 and 8 million tons in the future. The measures taken will also increase the economic activation of the port of Gliwice and contribute to the promotion of inland waterway transport in Poland. It should be stressed that the investments planned by the Ministry in the development of inland waterways will not only increase the importance of inland waterway transport, but will also allow, inter alia, for the development of inland waterways. a more extensive tourism movement. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u422	Dear Marshal, (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! The introduction of a basic system of hospital care provision, hereinafter referred to as a security system, under the Law of 23 March, in addition to the ordering of the system, in addition to determining the responsibilities and thus establishing certain directions for development specific to the different levels of the security system, will bring real benefits to patients, and you asked that, the patient is each of us. It will bring real benefits in terms of improving the coordination and continuity of patient care, the more rational management of healthcare resources, the stability of funding for those hospitals that enter the system of security - and thus improving the conditions for investing in their development - to adapt the internal structure of medicinal entities to current health needs. Within the framework of the security scheme, six security levels have been identified. These levels are defined by an indication of the hospital treatment profiles that characterize them in hospitalization mode. This is important because only hospitalization mode is included in the security system. The scheduled hospitalisation mode and day-to-day hospitalization mode are located outside of this security system and, as far as they are concerned, will be purchased by competitive offers. Separation of WW. the level of the security system reflects for the first three hospitals, namely I, II and III, a certain degree of gradation in terms of the number of profiles implemented and their degree of specialisation. Two more, i.e. oncological and pulmonological, were separated because of the specifics of the benefits they are dedicated to. On the other hand, the separation of the nationwide level serves to recognise the special role of national hospitals, clinical hospitals or hospitals created by ministers. Pursuant to the Act and on the basis of the Regulation of the Minister of Health of 13 June on the establishment of detailed criteria for the eligibility of healthcare providers to the level of the security system, the directors of the voivodship branches of the National Health Fund announced on 27 June a list of hospitals qualified for the protection system in each voivodship. A total of 594 medical establishments, including 78 non-public establishments, were eligible for the security scheme. It is a certain reference to what the honourable Member has pointed out, because the relevant non-public actors are included in the security system. And so: The first degree of protection - in total the number of medical establishments, hospitals are 283 entities, non-public are 52 entities, the second degree - 96 hospitals, non-public 10, the third degree - 62 hospitals, non-public five, the oncological level - 20 hospitals, non-public six, the degree of pulmonological - 30 hospitals, non-public four, and finally paediatric - 13 hospitals, non-public one, and the level of nationwide - 90 hospitals. Thus, the eligibility for the security scheme did not take into account who was the host body, but the implementation of certain criteria defined in the Act, as well as the regulation of the Minister of Health. You asked the Member what could happen within three months. First of all, it must be said that from 1 July to 30 September agreements were annexed in the field of hospital treatment, in the field of medical rehabilitation, as well as outpatient specialist care, and contracts in the field of outpatient specialist care, which do not enter the security system, will be annexed by the middle of next year, i.e. by 30 June 2018. An amendment which is possible during these 3 months, which is provided for by the Act, is the possibility of inclusion in the list of connected entities. If the entity - the lead authorities or one authority - has taken the act of merger and has submitted the act of merger by 20 June and by 21 September this year. present a discharge from the register of providers of medical activities, then this combined entity will be included in the qualification list. In that sense, it is possible to change. In addition, the change is possible - and this has already happened in a number of points - if errors are found. There were situations, for example. It wasn't a full name. This was the case for several products, several drug programs. At the moment, under the Act, the appeal procedure is in progress, which will be completed by 27 July this year. First, non-qualified entities or those which have considered that they have been qualified to the wrong level or, for example, they have not qualified all the scopes or groups of benefits, they may appeal against the decision of the Director. Then, if this decision is not taken into account, they may appeal to the President of the National Health Fund. So in such a way, there may be changes here, although it seems that they will only occur in terms of, say, some minor errors that have occurred. It is worth noting that 104 persons who appealed against the selection decisions benefited from this appeal tool. At the moment, branch directors are preparing to respond to these appeals. An important important element of the security system is that one contract covers three categories of benefits, or actually four categories of benefits, because it covers hospital care, includes outpatient specialist care in hospital clinics, includes medical rehabilitation in fixed conditions or day care, includes night and holiday health care in cases where the hospital had a contract, so they are included in the list in the emergency room or hospital emergency department. This solution should significantly improve the coordination, continuity and comprehensive care of the patient. It can also be expected that, in many cases, this will reduce waiting time to a specialist clinic and hospital treatment, as a lump sum form of benefit financing will allow providers to adapt and operate more flexibly to the real needs of their patients, since the current model of contracting benefits promotes behaviour to adapt the internal organisational structure of hospitals primarily on the basis of the clearing products purchased by the payer. It is also worth saying that flat-rate settlement is only one tool. The second tool is the financing of separately financed benefits, which is carried out on the basis of the list of benefits indicated in the Health Minister's Regulation. This list includes 24 benefits from both outpatient treatment and hospital care. The Member asks for information, in particular information for patients, what changes for patients in this system. It is worth saying that, in formal terms, as well as the rules for providing benefits to patients, nothing changes. The patient in the system is moving the same way. By way of organisational changes in the system, however, it is possible to coordinate the continuity of the provision of services, for example through a treatment plan, because as a patient enters the system in the field of outpatient care then hospital treatment or then rehabilitation treatment, it is this mechanism of financing and combining several scopes in one contract that offers an opportunity to look at the patient as a whole. Of course, much will depend on hospital management, but this system projection also increases confidence in hospital management, because it will depend on their direct decisions on how they use their resources, funds and how they respond to patients' needs. It must also be said that in terms of keeping information about the security system [[♪ The bell ♪]] on the website of the Ministry of Health was placed the appropriate website, while the Minister of Health, as well as the employees of the Ministry of Health, including myself, have been meeting in all sixteen voivodships for the past months...

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u468	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! First of all, I wanted to declare that we will answer questions that are not directly related to the subject of this debate in writing because the scope of doctor training is whether doctors are actually leaving and whether they have stopped leaving, the scope of resident training goes beyond the content of this discussion and we will answer these questions in writing. It also concerns the minimum level of remuneration and qualifications that are related to this minimum level of remuneration - and there was one question - and we will answer that in writing. Anyway, the project was properly processed. On the other hand, I will try to answer questions and doubts which were directly related to the subject of this debate. Maybe I should start with Mr Szczerby, who asked specifically about geriatrics. I would like to say that geriatrics are included in the third-degree hospital security system, in national hospitals and in those profiles that are alien clinics. This applies to about... actually exactly 18 hospitals that performed benefits in this profile. In addition, on the basis of the Regulation of the Minister of Health on the establishment of specific eligibility criteria for I or II hospitals may account for geriatrics through the internal disease profile and this covers 28 hospitals. A total of 46 hospitals were therefore included in the security scheme that concerns geriatrics. You mentioned the Wolski Hospital, but perhaps it is worth saying that such a situation of inclusion of geriatrics in the security system could only apply to those profiles that have had a contract for at least the last 2 calendar years. The hospital you named didn't meet these criteria, unfortunately, and that's why it wasn't included in the security system. In Mazowsze, a total of five profiles did not meet these criteria, and therefore the director of the Mazowieckie branch will announce competitions for geriatrics in Warsaw and more. At the same time, it must be added that the Minister of Health looks at geriatrics in this perspective. We treat this in such a way that the elderly should try to take care of them properly in their living environment. Staying in an elderly hospital should be kept to the minimum necessary. Moreover, an elderly patient can be treated in all wards, because he can eventually have, like all of us - with certain specifics - various diseases. Treatment of older people should not be identified with hospital treatment, geriatrics should be primarily consultative. A geriatric doctor should be available as a consultant for every doctor in the hospital, as well as open medicine. Geriatric units should be primarily scientific and educational. Indeed, it is true that we have few geriatrics and we need to educate geriatrics, but that is why it is particularly important that geriatric centres be financed in scientific and educational centres. Therefore, the key role for the development of geriatrics in Poland is the education of an adequate number of specialists in this field, with the right direction, as I mentioned, developing geriatric branches mainly in hospitals associated with medical universities. However, it should be mentioned that already under the Regulation of the Minister for Health of 22 November 2013 on hospital care guaranteed benefits, the provider of hospitalisation and planned hospitalisation benefits is required to develop and implement geriatric procedures for the patient, excluding of course those hospitals which have completely different profiles, i.e. paediatric, neonatological or obstetric profiles. Therefore, this procedure should be carried out in a hospital regardless of the fact that the medical entity of the geriatric branch is in the structure. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-07-sejm-45-2.u472	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Whitegard is a 24-thousand city in Central Pomerania affected by typical regional problems - high unemployment and resulting emigration to other centres in Poland and abroad. However, if the problems are typical, the way the municipality counteracts them is quite unusual and innovative. On 25 February 2015, the Whitegard Councillors passed an act of local law to keep young people in the city. The resolution on the first annual fee and fee for the use of perpetual real estate is the idea of Mayor Krzysztof Baginski presented to the citizens of Białogard in the election campaign. A person who is under the age of 35 can buy a building plot with a bonus of 80% , paying only $3,000 for it. PLN and annual tax of PLN 200. If the same person wanted to buy a lot in normal mode, she'd pay between 15 and 20,000. PLN for the first fee and would pay 1 000. PLN annual fee for a period of 99 years. In practice, the citizen acquires a land for permanent use at 20% of its market price. Instead of paying a total of 115 thousand. PLN, will pay a maximum of 22,23 thousand. zł. A happy buyer has to build a house on it in four years. If it doesn't work out, he's got to return the bonus. The white-garded system, because that's what it's called, is known as the Young's Department. It has two main advantages: it is cheap and connects young people with the place of residence, the city of Białogard. The city gradually designates new plots for young people to tender. These are not large quantities, because the system consists in implementing the principle of grain grain, and a measure will be collected. Systematicity and consistency, spread over time, providing the municipalities with constant operating and development conditions, not changing the rules during the game may have an unexpected effect over several years. Białogard and the mayor of this city Krzysztof Bagiński show and give an example of Poland as a whole, that young people can be encouraged to stay in the city and in the country, without state aid and external funds, with only their own capabilities. I think the example of whiteguard is worth spreading across the country. Let us imitate the city of Białogard in the Middle Pomerania, which decided not to wait for the sympathy of the central authorities and to take matters into its own hands. Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, Remember the name of this city. It's Whitegard. [[Applause]]
